Let's design final code robust:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def make_uniform(n):
    return [1.0] * max(1, int(n))

def get_prior_sequence():
    try:
        seq = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        return None
    if not isinstance(seq, list):
        return None
    # clip to [0,1000], ensure finite numeric
    out = []
    for x in seq:
        if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (int, float)):
            return None
        try:
            fx = float(x)
        except Exception:
            return None
        if not np.isfinite(fx):
            return None
        if fx < 0: fx=0.0
        if fx >1000: fx=1000.0
        out.append(fx)
    if not out: return None
    return out
```
But using np.isfinite on float ok.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s)-1.0)
    n = 6962
    prior = get_prior_sequence()
    if prior is not None:
         n = len(prior)
    candidates=[]
    # uniform of prior length or 6962
    candidates.append(make_uniform(n))
    if prior: candidates.append(prior)
    # maybe a few normalized variants? But no need.
    best = None; best_score = np.inf
    # evaluate with actual if possible
    for cand in candidates:
       try:
          s = float(evaluate_sequence(cand))
       except NameError:
          # if evaluator unavailable, use ones; but shouldn't
          s = np.inf
       except Exception:
          s = np.inf
       if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
           best_score=s; best=cand
    if best is None: best = make_uniform(n)
    print(f"Cauchy-Schwarz lower bound is 2. Current best score={best_score}")
    # Optional very brief perturbation search to catch any hidden/rounding improvement.
```

```
def _finite_number_list(seq, max_len=100000):
    if not isinstance(seq, list) or not seq or len(seq)>max_len: return None
    out=[]
    for x in seq:
       if isinstance(x,bool) or not isinstance(x,(int,float)): return None
       try: v=float(x)
       except: return None
       if not np.isfinite(v): return None
       out.append(v)
    return out
```

import numpy as np
import time

_DOC = """..."""

def make_uniform(length):
    length = max(1, int(length))
    return [1.0] * length

def clean_sequence(unclean):
    ... top-level

def safe_evaluate(cand):
    try:
      val = evaluate_sequence(cand)
      val = float(val)
    except Exception:
      return float('inf')
    if np.isfinite(val): return val
    return float('inf')

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """..."""
    print("Using Cauchy-Schwarz bound: score >= 2 for the supplied evaluator.")
    base_len = 6962
    # Try to use prior length if reasonable, but keep capped for evaluation time.
    try:
       prior = clean_sequence(height_sequence_1)
       if prior is not None:
          # We do not evaluate huge prior to avoid slow np.convolve.
          if len(prior) <= 12000:
             base_len = len(prior)
    except Exception:
       prior = None
    best = make_uniform(base_len)
    best_score = safe_evaluate(best)
    print(f"Uniform length {base_len} score = {best_score!r}")
    if prior is not None and len(prior) <= 12000:
       prior_score = safe_evaluate(prior)
       print(f"Prior score = {prior_score!r}")
       if prior_score < best_score:
           best_score = prior_score
           best = prior
    # Add a few uniform lengths? Maybe not. If base_len changed due prior, okay.
    # If best_score is somehow inf, use length 1.
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
       best = [1.0]
       best_score = safe_evaluate(best)
    print(f"Returning best length {len(best)} with score {best_score!r}.")
    return [float(x) for x in best]
```

If we include FFT fast score:
```
def fast_score(arr):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    n = len(arr)
    size = 1
    while size < 2*n-1: size <<=1
    f = np.fft.rfft(arr, size)
    ac = np.fft.irfft(f * np.conj(f), size)[:2*n-1]
    # ensure real, nonnegative-ish
    m = float(np.max(ac))
    if m < 0: m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s*s))
```

Let's deeply understand.

Given nonnegative sequence a length n. b = a * a linear convolution length 2n-1. Score = 2n max(b) / (sum a)^2. Scaling invariant. Uniform: b coefficients triangular: at center n (if n odd?) For [1]*n, conv coefficients: 1,2,...,n,...,1. max = n. sum=n. Score=2n*n/n^2=2. So uniform gives 2. But other sequences can get central convolution max lower than n? The average of b coefficients = sum(b) = (sum a)^2 = n^2 over 2n-1 coeffs, average ~n/2. So max at least n/2. Score lower bound by average: score >= 2n*(S^2/(2n-1))/S^2 = 2n/(2n-1) ~1. So target 1.5 plausible. Need minimize maximum coefficient of self-convolution of nonnegative sequence, normalized by S^2. This is like finding a nonnegative sequence with a flat "product" convolution, i.e. all pair sums over diagonals have equal sum. This is like constructing a sequence with low peak sum of products along anti-diagonals. Nonnegative, not signed. This is similar to "binary/autocorrelation"? It's convolution not correlation, but for self-convolution it's symmetric? Wait convolution of a with itself is not symmetric (as [1,2,3] gave [1,4,10,12,9]) but b_k and b_{2n-2-k} correspond to swapping order but product commutative: b_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}; b_{2n-2-k}=sum_i a_i a_{2n-2-k-i} = sum_j a_{2n-2-j} a_j = sum_j a_j a_{2n-2-j}. This is convolution of reversed? Not equal generally if a not palindromic. But max over all includes both.

Could choose sequence symmetric (palindromic) to make convolution symmetric? If a_i = a_{n-1-i}, then b_k = b_{2n-2-k}. Uniform symmetric. Maybe good to symmetrize.

Objective: minimize max over k of sum_i a_i a_{i+k?}. For convolution, shifts with sign. Let define a_0..a_{n-1}. b_k = sum_{i=max(0,k-(n-1))}^{min(n-1,k)} a_i a_{k-i}. For k from 0 to 2n-2. This is like all pairwise products grouped by index sum i+j=k. So it's the distribution of sums of indices of pairs in the product multiset. The zero-lag sum squares does not appear as a single coefficient unless sequence is reversed; linear convolution's coefficients are sums of pairs with index sum, so each a_i^2 contributes to different diagonal k=2i. Thus max can be below sum squares. For periodic large repetition, many a_i^2 contributions spread across cyclic diagonals, but the cyclic convolution zero diagonal modulo m includes all pairs i≡j mod m across periods, summing r∑ p_i^2, so c0=∑p_i^2. So linear core approximates cyclic, and c0 peak = sum squares per period. For dense periodic, uniform is min. Therefore periodic dense cannot beat 2? But target 1.5 maybe requires finite-edge effects: linear convolution doesn't wrap, so a_i^2's are separated and don't all concentrate. We need use the finite path structure, not cyclic.

Let's formulate block optimization. Let sequence a of length N, divided into B blocks of length L (N=BL). Block values h_j≥0. Convolution coefficients b_k = ∑_{i,j} h_i h_j * count_{ij,k}, where count is number of pairs of positions in block i,j summing to k. For k, if i,j block indices, counts are trapezoidal. We can compute b for all k (length 2N-1) efficiently by np.convolve of expanded sequence, or by convolving block sequence with triangular weights? For B small, expand N maybe 4096 and compute FFT. Its self-convolution = (h*h) convolved with ones_L ones_L? Specifically a = H * J (Kronecker). a*a = (H*H) * (J*J) where * is convolution at block level? If blocks equal length L, the sequence is a block-level sequence H with each symbol replicated L times. Convolution coefficient at offset k = L*block_offset + r. We can implement numerical optimization using feasible M and active set. Let's design a robust algorithm that likely gets below 1.51 within time.

Approach options:
1. Start with a good parametric family, e.g. piecewise linear/quadratic or recursive blocks, use scipy to optimize for target length n (like 4096/6962). Compute exact score with FFT. Then refine with multiplicative updates / projected gradient.
2. Direct nonnegative sequence optimization via gradient (softmax) and line search, using FFT for objective and gradient. This can adapt full length.

Let's explore direct optimization in detail.

Let n fixed maybe 4096 or 6962. Normalize sum=1. Variables p positive. Objective F(p)=max_k b_k. Another constructive route: Since we can choose weights not just binary, solve the optimization numerically. No need analytic. We can implement a general optimizer using `scipy.optimize` on a moderate-dimensional representation (e.g. cubic spline coefficients) and then refine. Let's focus on that.

Representation: Let p_i be generated by applying a nonnegative transformation to a smooth base function. For example, p_i ∝ exp(η (u_i)) where u_i is a low-degree polynomial or combination of Legendre polynomials (nonlinear ensures positivity). Sum normalized. Optimize coefficients to minimize smooth max of convolution. Use gradient computed via FFT. This can capture asymmetric shapes.

Projected gradient with line search:
```
p = initial normalized
for it in range(max_iter):
  b, M = fft_conv(p)
  G0 = smooth_max(b,beta)
  w = softmax(beta*b)
  s = corr(p,w)
  g = 2*s
  grad = g - g.mean()  # for simplex
  # step size candidate
  for alpha in alphas:
      q = project_simplex(p - alpha*grad)
      G1 = smooth_max(conv(q))
      if G1 < G0: break
  if no improvement, reduce global alpha
  p=q
```

Let's test projected gradient concept more concretely. Suppose p uniform, β finite. b triangular: b_k = c_k/n^2. c_k = min(k+1,n,2n-k-1). M at center. w_k proportional exp(β c_k/n^2). The gradient g_i=2∑ p_j w_{i+j}. Since p_j=1/n, g_i=(2/n) A_i where A_i=∑_{j: i+j valid} w_{i+j}. A_i sums w over a window of diagonals from i to i+n-1. For center i, window includes high weights, A_i large; near edges, window includes lower triangular values? Maybe g_i larger in center. Subtract mean and project subtracts more from center, increasing edges? This would make p more U-shaped, possibly bad. But softmax weights maybe want to reduce central peak by moving mass to edges? Block L-BFGS objective function details:
```
def block_param_objective(c, n, B, beta, n_scale, full? maybe):
    L=n//B
    z = np.empty(B); z[:-1]=c; z[-1]=0
    z -= np.max(z)
    wblock = np.exp(z); S = wblock.sum(); wblock /= S
    H = wblock / L # full sum 1
    # Build p full? For FFT conv, need p length n. Could repeat H.
    p = np.repeat(H, L)
    b = fft_conv(p)
    G,w = smooth_max(b, beta)
    Gopt = n * G
    # gradient:
    s = correlation(p,w)
    g = 2*s
    # aggregate GsumB by blocks: g blocks are length L. Use reshape(B,L).sum(axis=1)
    Gsum = g.reshape(B, L).sum(axis=1)
    Wavg = np.dot(wblock, Gsum)
    gradz = (wblock / L) * (Gsum - Wavg)
    grad = n * gradz[:-1]  # variables first B-1
    return Gopt, grad
```
Check derivative scaling: p_i=H_b, derivative w.r.t z_q has 1/L. Gopt includes n scale. Good. If use p sum1, G ~ maxb ~1/n, Gopt~1. Gradient Gopt ~n*gradz. gradz ~ (w/L)*(Gsum - Wavg). Gsum ~ L*average g ~ L*1/n =1/L? Actually g ~1/n, sum L gives 1. w~1/B, /L ~1/(BL)=1/N, gradz~1/N, n*gradz~n/N=1? Since N=n, grad~O(1). Good for L-BFGS.

Full projected gradient function:
```
def run_full_refine(p, deadline, beta=1e6, max_iter=2000, verbose=True):
   p = clean normalize sum1
   best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
   alpha_mult=1.0
   for it in range(max_iter):
      if time > deadline: break
      b=fft_conv(p)
      G,w=smooth_max(b,beta)
      Gval = n*G
      s=corr(p,w); g=2*s
      grad = g - np.mean(g)
      gnorm = np.linalg.norm(grad, ord=2)
      if gnorm < 1e-12: break
      # normalize? Use grad as is; step candidates scale = alpha_mult * (n * np.mean(np.abs(grad)))? Hmm.
      base = alpha_mult * 0.5 # since grad O(1/n), p O(1/n), step 0.5 maybe okay? If grad O(1/n), p - 0.5 grad changes by ~0.5/n, similar p. Actually p_i~1/n=1.2e-4, grad~1/n, so 0.5*grad~0.6/n, comparable and can make negative; projection handles. Try [1.0,0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001]*alpha_mult.
      accepted=False
      for alpha in candidates:
         q=project_simplex(p - alpha*grad)
         qb=fft_conv(q)
         qmax=float(np.max(qb)); qscore=2*n*qmax
         if qscore < best_score - 1e-12 and qscore < Gval? maybe accept if qscore < current exact.
         # Track exact current: cur_score=2*n*max(b)
         if qscore < cur_score - 1e-12:
             p=q; cur_score=qscore; if qscore<best_score: best...; accepted=True; break
      if not accepted:
         alpha_mult *=0.5
         if alpha_mult < 1e-6: break
      else:
         if not improved best: alpha_mult=min(2.0? maybe keep)
   return best_p,best_score
```

FFT convolution and correlation correctness:
`fft_conv(a)`: p length n, size >= 2n-1. F*F. For real a, irfft returns real length size. The first 2n-1 coefficients linear convolution. Good. Since order is convolution a_i a_j with i+j=k. Yes.
`correlation(p,w)`:
We want s_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} p_j w_{i+j}; w length m=2n-1. Let dr = w[::-1] (length m), index r: dr[t]=w[m-1-t]. Convolution C = p * dr: C[k]=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} p_j dr[k-j] if 0≤k-j<m -> w[m-1-(k-j)] = w[m-1-k+j]. For term to equal w[i+j], need m-1-k=i -> k=m-1-i. So s_i=C[m-1-i]. So s = C[m-1 : m-1-n+1 : -1] (length n). In Python, if `C` length at least m, indices: `C[m-1 : m-1-n : -1]`? Example n=2,m=3,m-1-n+1=1, slice C[2:1:-1] gives C[2],C[1] (stop exclusive 1), length2. If using `C[m-1 : m-1-n : -1]` stop=m-1-n? For n=2, stop=1, gives 2,1 good. For n=3,m=5, need indices 4,3,2. slice C[4:1:-1] gives 4,3,2. Formula stop=m-1-n =4-3=1? Actually m=5,m-1-n=1, yes stop exclusive 1. So `s = C[m-1 : m-1-n : -1].copy()`. Good.
But C length from FFT may be larger; we take first n+m-1 coefficients. The slice indices within. Good.

```
"""
Algorithm summary: The objective is 2n max(a*a)/(sum a)^2. Normalize sum to 1, minimize max self-convolution. We use a smooth-max L-BFGS optimization over piecewise-constant block log-weights, then a full-length projected gradient refinement using FFT convolutions.
"""

import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

BETA = 1_000_000.0
N_DEFAULT = 8192

def smax_and_w(b,beta): ...

def corr_p_w(p,w): ...

```

Implementation details:
`fft_conv`:
```
def fft_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0]*a[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    size=1
    need=2*n-1
    while size < need: size <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,size)[:need]
    # clean
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b)
    # If tiny negatives due to roundoff, set to 0
    # But if significant negative due bug? take max with 0.
    if b.size:
        b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return b
```
Setting negative to 0 might slightly bias gradient? Used in objective; okay. For gradient, w based on nonnegative b. Good.

`smax_and_w`:
```
def smax_and_w(b,beta):
    b = np.asarray(b)
    if b.size==0:
        return np.inf, b.copy()
    m=float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf, np.zeros_like(b)
    if m <= 0:
        return 0.0, np.ones_like(b)/b.size
    if beta <=0:
        return m, np.ones_like(b)/b.size
    x = beta*(b-m)
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    Z=float(np.sum(e))
    if Z <=0 or not finite:
       e = np.zeros_like(b); e[int(np.argmax(b))]=1.0; Z=1.0
    w = e/Z
    G = m + np.log(Z)/beta
    return float(G), w
```
If beta very large and m=0, handle.

`corr_p_w`:
```
def corr_p_w(p,w):
    n=p.size; m=w.size
    need=n+m-1
    if n==0 or m==0: return np.zeros(n)
    size=1
    while size<need: size<<=1
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,size)
    WR=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],size)
    C=np.fft.irfft(P*WR,size)[:need]
    s = C[m-1 : m-1-n : -1]  # stops before m-1-n
    return np.ascontiguousarray(s)
```
Check stop: For n=1,m=1: need1, C length1, m-1=0, m-1-n=-1; slice C[0:-1:-1] gives [C0]? In Python, start0 stop-1 step-1: includes index0? Let's test mentally: `C[0:-1:-1]` with length1: start 0, stop -1 (which is index0? exclusive), step -1; from 0 to 0 exclusive? It may return [C0]? For negative step, condition index > stop. stop -1 corresponds len-1=0; index 0 >0 false, so empty? Need be careful. Use explicit: `s = C[m-1 : m-1-n : -1]` for n=1: m=1, m-1-n=-1. In Python, stop=-1 means last index, for negative step from 0 down to > -1? index0 > -1 true, then next -1 > -1 false, so includes 0? Stop interpreted as -1 (abs len? with len>1?). For length1, stop -1 resolved to 0? Actually slice with negative stop: if stop <0, stop += len. len=1, stop=-1+1=0. Condition for negative step: index > stop. 0 >0 false => empty. So n=1 empty. We can handle n=1 separately: return w.copy? For p length1, s_0=w_0? i=0,j=0 => w0. `C[0]`. Use if n==1: return np.array([float(np.dot(p,w[:1]))]). More generally, use positive indices: `start = m-1; stop = m-n` because m-1-n+1 = m-n. For slice with step -1, stop exclusive. We need indices start down to stop inclusive where stop = m-n. For n=1,m=1: start0, stop0; `C[0:0:-1]` empty. For negative step, to include stop, stop must be less than included last. In Python, `C[start:stop-1:-1]` includes stop if stop>0. But if stop=0, can't simple. Easier: `s = C[m-n : m][::-1]`? Let's check n=2,m=3: m-n=1, C[1:3] = [C1,C2], reversed [C2,C1] good. n=1,m=1: C[0:1]=[C0], reversed [C0]. n=3,m=5: m-n=2, C[2:5]=[C2,C3,C4], reversed [C4,C3,C2] good. So use `s = C[m-n : m][::-1].copy()`. Great.
```
`need` may be larger than m? C length need. `m-n` nonnegative? m=2n-1, n>0 => m-n=n-1 ≥0. Good.
`corr` for n=1 works.
```

`project_simplex`: Use `np.maximum` and sum. If v has nan, return uniform.
```
def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
       return np.full(v.shape, target/v.size) if v.size else v
    if v.size==0: return v
    low=float(np.min(v))-1.0
    high=float(np.max(v))+1.0
    for _ in range(60):
       mid=0.5*(low+high)
       s=float(np.sum(np.maximum(v-mid,0.0)))
       if s>target:
           low=mid
       else:
           high=mid
    tau=0.5*(low+high)
    q=np.maximum(v-tau,0.0)
    s=q.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite:
       q=np.full(v.shape,target/v.size)
    else:
       q *= target/s
    return q
```
If target=1 and v huge, okay.

`normalize_simplex`: `p=np.maximum(p,0); s=p.sum(); if s<=0 uniform; p/=s`.

`block_to_p`:
```
def block_to_p(n,B,c):
    L=n//B
    z=np.empty(B)
    z[:-1]=c
    z[-1]=0.0
    z -= np.max(z)
    wb=np.exp(z)
    wb /= wb.sum()
    H=wb/L
    return np.repeat(H, n//L? B*L) # n
```
If B*L != n? Choose B divides N. For full search, N=8192 and B powers. Use B list that divide.

`block_objective`:
Need `n` passed; B; beta. It builds p. If `n % B !=0`, skip.
```
def block_objective(c,n,B,beta):
    L = n//B
    z = np.empty(B)
    z[:-1]=c; z[-1]=0.0
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)): return np.inf, np.zeros_like(c)
    z = z - np.max(z)
    wb = np.exp(z)
    ssum = float(wb.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(ssum) or ssum <=0: return np.inf,...
    wb /= ssum
    H = wb/L
    p = np.repeat(H, L) # length B*L = n if divisible
    b = fft_conv(p)
    G,w = smax_and_w(b,beta)
    if not np.isfinite(G): return np.inf, zeros
    s = corr_p_w(p,w)
    g = 2.0*s
    Gsum = g.reshape(B,L).sum(axis=1)
    Wavg = float(np.dot(wb, Gsum))
    gradz = (wb / L) * (Gsum - Wavg)
    grad = n * gradz[:-1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return n*G, grad
```
Potential: If B=1? We won't. L may be 1 for B=N, not used maybe. For B=1, c empty, derivative? skip.

In `param_block_search`, for each B, if B does not divide N? Choose B from [8,16,32,64,128] all divide 8192. Good. B=128 variables127; L-BFGS maybe okay. To save time, B up to 64 or 128 depending budget. We can set if budget large: [8,16,32,64,128]. Each minimization maybe 50 maxiter. Initials maybe 3. Time. We need avoid too long before full refine. Set param budget e.g. min(600, 0.35*budget). With N=8192, evaluations cheap, 600s enough.

Initial c generation top-level:
```
def block_initial_list(B, rng, count=4):
   inits=[]
   inits.append(np.zeros(B-1))
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,max(1,B-1))
   for a in (-3.0, 3.0):
       c=a*(x-0.5)
       inits.append(c)
   # two block
   c=np.zeros(B-1)
   if B>2:
      c[:B//2] = 1.5
      c[B//2:] = -1.5
   inits.append(c)
   # random
   for _ in range(3): inits.append(0.7*rng.normal(0,1,B-1))
   return inits
```
But c signs: x-0.5, negative left if a positive? Doesn't matter.
For two-block with last fixed 0, if c set last -1.5 but last is variable? c length B-1, last element (block B-2) maybe part of second half. OK.

def param_block_search(n, deadline, rng, beta=BETA):
    best_p = np.full(n,1.0/n)
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)
    Blist = [8,16,32,64,128]
    if time budget small adapt: if budget_s < 600, Blist [8,16,32]
    for B in Blist:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       inits = block_initial_list(B,rng, count=3)
       bounds = [(-6.0,6.0)]*(B-1)
       for c0 in inits:
          if time > deadline: break
          # maybe time per call 10s? Use maxiter
          start_time = time.monotonic()
          res = minimize(_block_objective, c0, args=(n,B,beta), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':80, 'ftol':1e-9, 'gtol':1e-6, 'maxls':50, 'maxcor':10})
          c = res.x if finite else c0
          p=block_to_p(n,B,c)
          score=fast_score(p)
          if score<best_score: update
          print progress
    return best_p,best_score
```
L-BFGS bounds: If initial outside, it projects. `c0` may be 1.5 within.
Could add `callback` to stop if time? But top-level no closures? We can pass a top-level callback? It would need state; not necessary; maxiter 80.
If `res.fun` finite but `res.x` not, skip.
Use `res.x` as c. The objective uses c as z[:-1]; if L-BFGS stays in bounds.

Potential issue: The block objective's smooth max with beta=1e6 and L-BFGS may have gradient zero if objective flat due invariance? No flat removed. But the objective is scale-free? p sum1; c parameter covers relative weights. Good.

`full_refine` details: Use `fast_score` and `smax`.
```
def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta=BETA, max_iter=20000):
    p=normalize_simplex(p0)
    # add tiny noise if all same? use rng noise level 1e-6
    if np.allclose(p, p[0], rtol=1e-12, atol=1e-12):
       p = p * (1.0 + 1e-5*np.sin(np.arange(len(p))*0.37))
       p=normalize_simplex(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    step=0.5
    plateau=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       b=fft_conv(p)
       G,w=smax_and_w(b,beta)
       if not finite break
       cur_smooth = len(p)*G
       cur_score = 2*len(p)*float(np.max(b))
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-15: best...
       s=corr_p_w(p,w)
       g=2*s
       grad = g - float(np.mean(g))
       gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
       if gnorm < 1e-13:
          break
       accepted=False
       # base step: use p_scale = float(np.mean(p)) maybe
       base = step
       for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001):
           alpha = base*mult
           q = project_simplex(p - alpha*grad, 1.0)
           qb=fft_conv(q)
           m2=float(np.max(qb))
           G2,_=smax_and_w(qb,beta)
           if len(p)*G2 < cur_smooth - 1e-13:
               p=q; cur_smooth=len(p)*G2; cur_score=2*len(p)*m2; accepted=True
               if cur_score < best_score: best...
               break
       if not accepted:
           step *=0.5
           plateau +=1
           if step < 1e-6 or plateau > 200: break
       else:
           # slightly increase if many accepts? keep or increase capped
           if plateau>0:
               step = min(step*2.0, 5.0)
           plateau=0
    return best_p,best_score
```

Let's design `make_structured_p(n, rng, name)`. Top-level:
```
def structured_starts(n, rng, limit=5):
    starts=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    for a in (-2.0,-1.0,1.0,2.0):
       z=a*(x-0.5)
       p=np.exp(z); p/=p.sum(); starts.append(p)
    # two block eps
    for eps in (0.3,0.6,0.9):
       p=np.ones(n); p[:n//2]=1+eps; p[n//2:]=1-eps; p=np.maximum(p,1e-6); p/=p.sum(); starts.append(p)
    # random smooth Fourier
    for _ in range(2):
       size=np.fft.next_fast_len(n)
       zc=np.fft.ifft(rng.normal(size=2?)+1j*rng.normal(size=2?))
       z=np.asarray(np.real(zc[:n]),float); z -= z.mean(); z = 1.0*np.tanh(z); p=np.exp(z); p/=p.sum(); starts.append(p)
    return starts
```
Need `rng.normal(size=size)` okay. `np.fft.next_fast_len` may exist; if not use 1; but numpy has. We can just size=1<<ceil. Use simple.

    _HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY = False

BETA_DEFAULT = 1_000_000.0

def _is_finite_float(x): ... maybe not needed.

Let's implement `param_block_search`:
```
def param_block_search(n, deadline, rng, beta=BETA_DEFAULT):
    best_p = np.full(n, 1.0/n, dtype=float)
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)
    if n <= 1: return best_p,best_score
    if _HAVE_SCIPY:
        B_list = []
        for B in (8,16,32,64,128):
            if B < n and n % B ==0:
                B_list.append(B)
        # maybe omit 128 if deadline small
        for B in B_list:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
            inits = block_initial_list(B,rng)
            bounds = [(-6.0,6.0)] * (B-1)
            for c0 in inits:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
                try:
                    c0 = np.asarray(c0,float)
                    c0 = np.clip(c0, -6.0, 6.0)
                    res = minimize(block_objective, c0, args=(n,B,beta), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':100, 'ftol':1e-10, 'gtol':1e-6, 'maxls':50, 'maxcor':10})
                    c = np.asarray(res.x, float)
                    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
                        c = c0
                    p = block_to_p(n,B,c)
                    sc = fast_score(p)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc; best_p = p.copy()
                    print(f"param B={B} init={id} score={best_score:.6f}")
                except Exception:
                    pass
    return best_p,best_score
```
`block_objective` expects c length B-1; if B=1 no. B list B<n. For n=8192, B up to128. 128 variables; L-BFGS memory okay. Could include 256? Too many; B=128 L=64. Good.

def block_initial_list(B,rng):
   list=[]
   if B<=1: return [np.empty(0)]
   list.append(np.zeros(B-1))
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,B-1)
   for a in (-3.0,3.0): list.append(a*(x-0.5))
   c=np.zeros(B-1)
   if B>2:
      half=(B-1)//2
      c[:half]=2.0; c[half:]=-2.0
   list.append(c)
   # sinusoidal
   t=np.linspace(0,2*np.pi,B-1)
   list.append(1.0*np.cos(t))
   list.append(0.7*rng.normal(0,1,B-1))
   list.append(1.2*rng.normal(0,1,B-1))
   return list
```

def structured_starts(n,rng,max_count=6):
   starts=[]
   p=np.full(n,1.0/n); starts.append(p)
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   for a in (-2.0,-1.0,1.0,2.0):
      z=a*(x-0.5); p=np.exp(z); p/=p.sum(); starts.append(p)
   for eps in (0.3,0.6):
      p=np.ones(n); if n>=2: p[:n//2]=1+eps; p[n//2:]=max(1e-3,1-eps); p=np.maximum(p,1e-6); p/=p.sum(); starts.append(p)
   # random smooth
   size=1
   while size<n: size*=2
   for _ in range(2):
      z=np.real(np.fft.ifft((rng.normal(size=2*size)+1j*rng.normal(size=2*size))))[:n]
      z -= z.mean(); z = np.tanh(1.0*z)
      p=np.exp(z); p/=p.sum(); starts.append(p)
   return starts[:max_count]
```

```
def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta=BETA_DEFAULT, max_iter=200000):
    p = normalize_simplex(p0)
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return p, np.inf
    best_p = p.copy(); best_score = fast_score(p)
    step = 1.0
    no_improve = 0
    it=0
    while it < max_iter and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it +=1
        b = fft_convolution(p)
        G,w = smooth_max_and_weights(b,beta)
        if not np.isfinite(G): break
        cur_smooth = float(n)*G
        m = float(np.max(b))
        cur_score = float(2)*n*m
        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score=cur_score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 500 and step < 1e-3: break
        s = corr_pw(p,w)
        g = 2.0*s
        grad = g - float(np.mean(g))
        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm < 1e-14:
            break
        # Optional: scale gradient? If gnorm very large, reduce step.
        base = step
        # We can set base = step * (1.0 / max(1.0, gnorm * n * 1e-3))? Hmm.
        accepted=False
        for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001):
            alpha = base * mult
            q = project_simplex(p - alpha*grad, 1.0)
            qb = fft_convolution(q)
            m2 = float(np.max(qb))
            G2,w2 = smooth_max_and_weights(qb,beta)
            if not np.isfinite(G2): continue
            if float(n)*G2 < cur_smooth - 1e-13:
                p = q
                cur_smooth = float(n)*G2
                cur_score = float(2)*n*m2
                accepted = True
                if cur_score < best_score - 1e-15:
                    best_score=cur_score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
                break
        if not accepted:
            step *= 0.5
            if step < 1e-7:
                break
        else:
            # grow slowly
            if step < 2.0:
                step = min(2.0, step*1.2)
            no_improve = 0
    return best_p, best_score
```

Let's draft code fully:

```
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

PARAM_BETA = 100000.0
FULL_BETA = 1000000.0

def fft_convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0]*a[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    need = 2*n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:need]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    # Clean tiny negative roundoff.
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return b
```
`np.ascontiguousarray(b, dtype=...)` works? `ascontiguousarray` has dtype? yes. If b already contiguous, returns. Good.

`smooth_max_and_weights`:
```
def smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float('inf'), np.empty(0)
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        z = np.zeros_like(b)
        return float('inf'), z
    if m <= 0.0 or beta <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones_like(b)
        w /= float(b.size)
        return m, w
    x = beta * (b - m)
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    Z = float(np.sum(e))
    if Z <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(Z):
        e = np.zeros_like(b)
        e[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        Z = 1.0
    w = e / Z
    G = m + float(np.log(Z)) / beta
    return float(G), w
```
If m=0, b all zero, weights uniform; okay.

`fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    if p.size==0: return inf
    S=float(np.sum(p))
    if S <= 0.0 or not finite: return inf
    b=fft_convolution(p)
    if b.size==0: return inf
    M=float(np.max(b))
    if not finite: return inf
    return float(2.0 * p.size * M / (S*S))
```

`corr_pw` as above.

`project_simplex`: Need handle target maybe not 1 for block H. We only use target 1, but implement.
```
def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size ==0: return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
       return np.full(v.shape, target / v.size, dtype=float)
    if target <=0: return np.zeros_like(v)
    low = float(np.min(v)) - 1.0
    high = float(np.max(v)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(50):
       mid = 0.5*(low+high)
       s = float(np.sum(np.maximum(v-mid, 0.0)))
       if s > target:
           low = mid
       else:
           high = mid
    tau = 0.5*(low+high)
    q = np.maximum(v - tau, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       q = np.full(v.shape, target / v.size, dtype=float)
    else:
       q *= target / s
    return q
```
If v has large negative, low fine.

`normalize_simplex`:
```
def normalize_simplex(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if p.size ==0: return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <=0 or not finite:
       return np.full(p.shape, 1.0/p.size)
    p *= 1.0/s
    return p
```

`block_to_p`:
```
def block_to_p(n,B,c):
    L = n // B
    z = np.empty(B, dtype=float)
    if B > 1:
       c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
       if c.size != B-1:
          tmp=np.zeros(B-1); m=min(len(tmp), len(c)); tmp[:m]=c[:m]; c=tmp
       z[:-1] = c
    z[-1] = 0.0
    z = z - np.max(z)
    wb = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(wb))
    if not finite or s<=0:
       wb = np.ones(B)/B
    else:
       wb /= s
    H = wb / float(L)
    return np.repeat(H, L)
```
If B divides n. If B=1, L=n, z[0]=0, wb=[1], H=1/n.

`block_objective`:
```
def block_objective(c,n,B,beta):
    L=n//B
    z=np.empty(B); z[:-1]=c; z[-1]=0.0 (if B=1 c empty)
    if not finite: return inf, zeros
    z=z-np.max(z)
    wb=np.exp(z); s=...; wb/=s
    H=wb/L
    p=np.repeat(H,L)
    b=fft_convolution(p)
    G,w=smooth_max_and_weights(b,beta)
    if not finite G: return inf, zeros
    s_vec=corr_pw(p,w)
    g=2.0*s_vec
    Gsum=g.reshape(B,L).sum(axis=1)
    Wavg=float(np.dot(wb,Gsum))
    gradz=(wb/float(L))*(Gsum-Wavg)
    grad=n*gradz[:-1]
    grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return float(n*G), grad.astype(float)
```
Need if B=1, gradz[:-1] empty. L-BFGS not with B=1.

`block_initial_list`:
```
def block_initial_list(B, rng):
    inits=[]
    if B <= 1: return [np.empty(0)]
    m=B-1
    inits.append(np.zeros(m))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    for a in (-3.0,3.0):
       inits.append(a*(x-0.5))
    c=np.zeros(m); half=m//2
    if half>0: c[:half]=2.0
    if m-half>0: c[half:]=-2.0
    inits.append(c)
    t=np.linspace(0.0,2.0*np.pi,m)
    inits.append(np.cos(t))
    inits.append(0.8*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,m))
    inits.append(1.5*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,m))
    return inits
```
For m=1, linspace [0], linear [0], two c [2? half=0, c[:0], c[0:] -2], cos[1? t=0], random. Good.

`param_block_search`:

def param_block_search(n, deadline, rng, max_B=128):
    best_p=np.full(n,1.0/n)
    best_score=fast_score(best_p)
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n < 8:
        return best_p,best_score
    B_candidates=[]
    for candidate in (8,16,32,64,128,256):
       if candidate <= max_B and candidate < n and n % candidate ==0:
          B_candidates.append(candidate)
    for B in B_candidates:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       inits=block_initial_list(B,rng)
       bounds=[(-6.0,6.0)]*(B-1)
       best_c = np.zeros(B-1)
       best_B_score = best_score
       # run stage1 and stage2? Let's do for inits then best_c with high? Maybe one stage with PARAM_BETA and optional second with best_c if time.
       for c0 in inits:
          if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
          try:
             c0=np.asarray(c0,float); c0=np.clip(c0,-6,6)
             res=minimize(block_objective,c0,args=(n,B,PARAM_BETA),method='L-BFGS-B',jac=True,bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':80,'ftol':1e-10,'gtol':1e-5,'maxls':40,'maxcor':10})
             c=np.asarray(res.x,float)
             if not finite: c=c0
             p=block_to_p(n,B,c)
             sc=fast_score(p)
             if finite and sc<best_B_score:
                best_B_score=sc; best_c=c
             print(f"block B={B} score={best_B_score:.6f}")
          except Exception:
             pass
       # second stage with higher beta from best_c if time
       if time.monotonic() < deadline and best_B_score < best_score - 1e-6:
          try:
             res=minimize(block_objective,best_c,args=(n,B,FULL_BETA/4.0),method='L-BFGS-B',jac=True,bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':80,'ftol':1e-10,'gtol':1e-5,'maxls':40,'maxcor':10})
             c=np.asarray(res.x,float)
             p=block_to_p(n,B,c)
             sc=fast_score(p)
             if finite and sc<best_B_score:
                best_B_score=sc
                p_copy=p.copy()
             else: p_copy=block_to_p(n,B,best_c)
          except: p_copy=block_to_p(n,B,best_c)
       else:
          p_copy=block_to_p(n,B,best_c)
       if best_B_score < best_score:
          best_score=best_B_score; best_p=p_copy.copy()
    return best_p,best_score
```

`run_block_minimize`:
```
def run_block_minimize(c0, n, B, beta, bounds):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return np.asarray(c0,float)
    c0=np.asarray(c0,float)
    if c0.size != B-1:
       tmp=np.zeros(B-1); m=min(c0.size,B-1); tmp[:m]=c0[:m]; c0=tmp
    c0=np.clip(c0,-6.0,6.0)
    options={'maxiter':80,'ftol':1e-10,'gtol':1e-5,'maxls':40,'maxcor':10}
    try:
       res = minimize(block_objective, c0, args=(n,B,beta), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options=options)
       c=np.asarray(res.x,float)
    except Exception:
       c=c0
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)): c=c0
    return np.clip(c,-6.0,6.0)
```

def choose_best_starts(starts, count):
    scored = []
    for p in starts:
        sc = fast_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append([sc, p])
    top = []
    for _ in range(count):
        if not scored:
            break
        bi = 0
        best_val = scored[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(scored)):
            if scored[i][0] < best_val:
                bi = i
                best_val = scored[i][0]
        top.append(scored[bi][1])
        del scored[bi]
    return top
```

```python
"""
Minimize 2*n*max(a*a)/(sum(a))^2 for nonnegative sequences.

The objective is scale invariant, so we normalize sum(a)=1 and minimize
max(a*a).  The program uses FFT-based convolutions and:

1. L-BFGS optimization over piecewise-constant block log-weights.
2. A brief full-length projected-gradient refinement.
3. A final official evaluate_sequence() check with a uniform fallback.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

LOW_BETA = 20000.0
HIGH_BETA = 200000.0
FULL_BETA = 1000000.0


def fft_convolution(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a, clipped to remove tiny FFT negative roundoff."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)

    need = 2 * n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1

    F = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)[:need]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)


def smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta):
    """Smooth max of b and normalized softmax weights."""
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf"), np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros_like(b, dtype=np.float64)

    if m <= 0.0 or beta <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones_like(b, dtype=np.float64)
        w /= float(b.size)
        return m, w

    x = beta * (b - m)
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    Z = float(np.sum(e))

    if Z <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(Z):
        e = np.zeros_like(b, dtype=np.float64)
        e[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        Z = 1.0

    w = e / Z
    G = m + float(np.log(Z)) / beta
    return float(G), w


def fast_score(p):
    """Fast local objective using FFT."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    S = float(np.sum(p))
    if S <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return float("inf")

    b = fft_convolution(p)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    M = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return float("inf")

    return float(2.0 * n * M / (S * S))


def corr_pw(p, w):
    """
    Return s_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, for i=0..n-1.
    This is used for the derivative of max(p*p) with softmax weights w.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    m = w.size
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(np.dot(p, w[:1]))], dtype=np.float64)

    need = n + m - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1

    P = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    WR = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    C = np.fft.irfft(P * WR, size)[:need]

    # s_i = C[m-1-i], i=0..n-1
    s = C[m - n : m][::-1].copy()
    return np.ascontiguousarray(s, dtype=np.float64)


def project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    """Project a vector onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=target}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    if target <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros_like(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        return np.full(v.shape, target / float(v.size), dtype=np.float64)

    low = float(np.min(v)) - 1.0
    high = float(np.max(v)) + 1.0

    for _ in range(50):
        mid = 0.5 * (low + high)
        s = float(np.sum(np.maximum(v - mid, 0.0)))
        if s > target:
            low = mid
        else:
            high = mid

    tau = 0.5 * (low + high)
    q = np.maximum(v - tau, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(q))

    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        q = np.full(v.shape, target / float(v.size), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        q *= target / s

    return q


def normalize_simplex(p):
    """Clip to nonnegative and normalize to sum 1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(p.shape, 1.0 / float(p.size), dtype=np.float64)
    p *= 1.0 / s
    return p


def block_to_p(n, B, c):
    """Convert relative block log-weights c (length B-1) to full sequence."""
    L = n // B
    z = np.empty(B, dtype=np.float64)
    if B > 1:
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        tmp = np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(tmp.size, c.size)
        tmp[:m] = c[:m]
        c = tmp
        z[:-1] = c
    z[-1] = 0.0

    z = z - np.max(z)
    wb = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(wb))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        wb = np.ones(B, dtype=np.float64) / float(B)
    else:
        wb /= s

    H = wb / float(L)
    return np.repeat(H, L)


def block_objective(c, n, B, beta):
    """
    Objective and gradient for block-constant sequences.
    The returned objective is n * smooth_max(p*p), so the scale is O(1).
    """
    L = n // B
    z = np.empty(B, dtype=np.float64)
    if B > 1:
        c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        tmp = np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(tmp.size, c.size)
        tmp[:m] = c[:m]
        c = tmp
        z[:-1] = c
    z[-1] = 0.0

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    z = z - np.max(z)
    wb = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(wb))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        wb = np.ones(B, dtype=np.float64) / float(B)
    else:
        wb /= s

    H = wb / float(L)
    p = np.repeat(H, L)

    b = fft_convolution(p)
    G, w = smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta)
    if not np.isfinite(G):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    svec = corr_pw(p, w)
    svec = np.maximum(svec, 0.0)
    g = 2.0 * svec

    Gsum = g.reshape(B, L).sum(axis=1)
    Wavg = float(np.dot(wb, Gsum))
    gradz = (wb / float(L)) * (Gsum - Wavg)

    grad = n * gradz[:-1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return float(n * G), grad.astype(np.float64)


def run_block_minimize(c0, n, B, beta, bounds):
    """Run L-BFGS-B on the block representation, robustly."""
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return np.asarray(c0, dtype=np.float64)

    c0 = np.asarray(c0, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if c0.size != max(0, B - 1):
        tmp = np.zeros(max(0, B - 1), dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(tmp.size, c0.size)
        tmp[:m] = c0[:m]
        c0 = tmp

    c0 = np.clip(c0, -6.0, 6.0)

    options = {
        "maxiter": 90,
        "ftol": 1e-10,
        "gtol": 1e-5,
        "maxls": 40,
        "maxcor": 10,
    }

    try:
        res = minimize(
            block_objective,
            c0,
            args=(n, B, beta),
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options=options,
        )
        c = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        c = c0

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        c = c0

    c = np.clip(c, -6.0, 6.0)
    return c


def block_initial_list(B, rng):
    """Several simple initial block weight directions."""
    inits = []
    if B <= 1:
        return [np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)]

    m = B - 1
    inits.append(np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
    for a in (-3.0, 3.0):
        inits.append(a * (x - 0.5))

    c = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    half = m // 2
    if half > 0:
        c[:half] = 2.0
    if m - half > 0:
        c[half:] = -2.0
    inits.append(c)

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, m, dtype=np.float64)
    inits.append(np.cos(t))
    inits.append(np.sin(t))

    inits.append(0.8 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, m))
    inits.append(1.5 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, m))

    return inits


def param_block_search(n, deadline, rng, max_B=128):
    """Optimize piecewise-constant log-weights at increasing resolution."""
    best_p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)

    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n < 8:
        return best_p, best_score

    candidates = []
    for B in (8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256):
        if B <= max_B and B < n and (n % B) == 0:
            candidates.append(B)

    for B in candidates:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        bounds = [(-6.0, 6.0)] * (B - 1)
        best_c = np.zeros(B - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        best_B_score = float("inf")

        inits = block_initial_list(B, rng)

        # Coarse smooth-max stage.
        for c0 in inits:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            c = run_block_minimize(c0, n, B, LOW_BETA, bounds)
            p = block_to_p(n, B, c)
            sc = fast_score(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_B_score:
                best_B_score = sc
                best_c = c.copy()

        # Sharper stage from the best coarse solution.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline and np.isfinite(best_B_score):
            c = run_block_minimize(best_c, n, B, HIGH_BETA, bounds)
            p = block_to_p(n, B, c)
            sc = fast_score(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_B_score:
                best_B_score = sc
                best_c = c.copy()
            p = block_to_p(n, B, best_c)
        else:
            p = block_to_p(n, B, best_c)

        if np.isfinite(best_B_score) and best_B_score < best_score:
            best_score = best_B_score
            best_p = p.copy()
            print(f"block B={B} best_score={best_score:.8f}")

    return best_p, best_score


def structured_starts(n, rng, max_count=8):
    """A few analytic/random starting profiles for full refinement."""
    starts = []

    p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    starts.append(p)

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    for a in (-2.5, -1.5, 1.5, 2.5):
        z = a * (x - 0.5)
        p = np.exp(z)
        p /= p.sum()
        starts.append(p)

    for eps in (0.35, 0.65):
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        if n >= 2:
            p[: n // 2] = 1.0 + eps
            p[n // 2 :] = max(1e-3, 1.0 - eps)
        p = np.maximum(p, 1e-8)
        p /= p.sum()
        starts.append(p)

    size = 1
    while size < n:
        size <<= 1

    for _ in range(2):
        zc = rng.normal(size=size) + 1j * rng.normal(size=size)
        z = np.real(np.fft.ifft(zc))[:n]
        z = z - float(np.mean(z))
        z = 1.5 * np.tanh(z)
        p = np.exp(z)
        p /= p.sum()
        starts.append(p)

    return starts[:max_count]


def choose_best_starts(starts, count):
    """Return up to count starts with the lowest fast_score."""
    scored = []
    for p in starts:
        sc = fast_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append([sc, p])

    top = []
    for _ in range(count):
        if not scored:
            break
        bi = 0
        for i in range(1, len(scored)):
            if scored[i][0] < scored[bi][0]:
                bi = i
        top.append(scored[bi][1])
        del scored[bi]

    return top


def full_refine(p0, deadline, rng, beta=FULL_BETA, max_iter=1000000):
    """Full-length projected-gradient refinement on the simplex."""
    p = normalize_simplex(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, float("inf")

    # Break perfect symmetry if starting at uniform.
    mean_p = float(np.mean(p))
    if n > 1 and float(np.max(np.abs(p - mean_p))) < 1e-13:
        t = 0.123 * np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
        p = normalize_simplex(p * (1.0 + 1e-4 * np.sin(t)))

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(p)

    step = 1.0
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while it < max_iter and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        b = fft_convolution(p)
        G, w = smooth_max_and_weights(b, beta)
        if not np.isfinite(G):
            break

        cur_smooth = float(n) * G
        m = float(np.max(b))
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * m)

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-15:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if it % 250 == 0:
            print(f"full refine it={it} best={best_score:.8f} step={step:.3e}")

        if no_improve > 2500:
            break

        svec = corr_pw(p, w)
        svec = np.maximum(svec, 0.0)
        g = 2.0 * svec
        grad = g - float(np.mean(g))

        gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm < 1e-14:
            break

        accepted = False
        base = step

        for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.002, 0.001):
            alpha = base * mult
            q = project_simplex(p - alpha * grad, 1.0)

            qb = fft_convolution(q)
            m2 = float(np.max(qb))
            G2, _ = smooth_max_and_weights(qb, beta)
            if not np.isfinite(G2):
                continue

            if float(n) * G2 < cur_smooth - 1e-13:
                p = q
                cur_smooth = float(n) * G2
                cur_score = float(2.0 * n * m2)
                accepted = True

                if cur_score < best_score - 1e-15:
                    best_score = cur_score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                    no_improve = 0

                break

        if not accepted:
            step *= 0.5
            if step < 1e-7:
                break
        else:
            if step < 2.0:
                step = min(2.0, step * 1.25)

    return best_p, best_score


def official_score(seq):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, with exception safety."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if np.isfinite(val):
        return val
    return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search function.

    Returns a list of nonnegative floats.  The search length is chosen as a
    power-of-two-ish size (8192 under normal budget) to balance attack surface
    and final O(n^2) evaluator cost.
    """
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42

    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    if budget_s <= 0.0:
        return [1.0]

    reserve = 90.0 if budget_s > 900.0 else 30.0
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, budget_s - reserve)

    if budget_s < 120.0:
        N = 2048
    elif budget_s < 600.0:
        N = 4096
    else:
        N = 8192

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_p = np.full(N, 1.0 / float(N), dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)
    print(f"initial N={N} score={best_score:.8f}")

    # ---- Phase 1: parametric block optimization ----
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 20.0:
        if budget_s >= 2000.0:
            max_B = 256
        elif budget_s >= 1000.0:
            max_B = 128
        else:
            max_B = 64

        param_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(60.0, 0.40 * budget_s))
        p_param, sc_param = param_block_search(N, param_deadline, rng, max_B=max_B)

        if np.isfinite(sc_param) and sc_param < best_score:
            best_score = sc_param
            best_p = p_param.copy()
            print(f"after param block search score={best_score:.8f}")

    # ---- Phase 2: full refinement from several good starts ----
    candidate_starts = [best_p]

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
        for p0 in structured_starts(N, rng, max_count=8):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 40.0:
                break
            candidate_starts.append(p0)

    top_starts = choose_best_starts(candidate_starts, 3)
    fracs = (0.55, 0.30, 0.15)

    for i, p0 in enumerate(top_starts):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0
        if remaining <= 20.0:
            break

        frac = fracs[i] if i < len(fracs) else 0.1
        alloc = remaining * frac
        sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + alloc

        p_fit, sc_fit = full_refine(p0, sub_deadline, rng, beta=FULL_BETA)
        if np.isfinite(sc_fit) and sc_fit < best_score:
            best_score = sc_fit
            best_p = p_fit.copy()
            print(f"full refine start {i} score={best_score:.8f}")

    # ---- Final cleanup and authoritative check ----
    final_p = normalize_simplex(best_p)
    final_p = np.maximum(final_p, 0.0)
    final_p = np.minimum(final_p, 1000.0)
    final_s = float(np.sum(final_p))
    if final_s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(final_s):
        final_p *= 1.0 / final_s

    final_seq = [float(x) for x in final_p]

    # The uniform sequence is a valid score-2 fallback.
    off = official_score(final_seq)
    if not np.isfinite(off) or off > 2.0 + 1e-9:
        return [1.0] * N

    return final_seq
```
